\displaystyle\boldsymbol{Y}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\left[(\boldsymbol{I}_{t-1}-\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}(\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}+\eta_{t}^{-1}\boldsymbol{I}_{d})^{-1}\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}})^{2}+\eta_{t}^{-1}\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}(\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}+\eta_{t}^{-1}\boldsymbol{I}_{d})^{-2}\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\right]\boldsymbol{Y}_{t-1}

\displaystyle+(y_{t}-\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{Y}_{t-1})^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{Y}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}[\boldsymbol{I}_{t-1}-2\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}(\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}+\eta_{t}^{-1}\boldsymbol{I}_{d})^{-1}\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}+\eta_{t}^{-1}\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}(\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}+\eta_{t}^{-1}\boldsymbol{I}_{d})^{-2}\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}(\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}+\eta_{t}^{-1}\boldsymbol{I}_{d})^{-1}\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}(\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}+\eta_{t}^{-1}\boldsymbol{I}_{d})^{-1}\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}]\boldsymbol{Y}_{t-1}+(y_{t}-\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{Y}_{t-1})^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{Y}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}[\boldsymbol{I}_{t-1}-2\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}(\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}+\eta_{t}^{-1}\boldsymbol{I}_{d})^{-1}\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}

\displaystyle\quad+\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}(\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}+\eta_{t}^{-1}\boldsymbol{I}_{d})^{-1}(\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}+\eta_{t}^{-1}\boldsymbol{I}_{d})(\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}+\eta_{t}^{-1}\boldsymbol{I}_{d})^{-1}\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}]Y_{t-1}+(y_{t}-\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{Y}_{t-1})^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{Y}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}[\boldsymbol{I}_{t-1}-\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}(\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}+\eta_{t}^{-1}\boldsymbol{I}_{d})^{-1}\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}]\boldsymbol{Y}_{t-1}+(y_{t}-\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{Y}_{t-1})^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{Y}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}(\boldsymbol{I}_{t-1}-\boldsymbol{H})\boldsymbol{Y}_{t-1}+(y_{t}-\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{Y}_{t-1})^{2}\kern 5.0pt.

(24)

Step 3:
Combine the results in the previous steps.

By (
B.5
) and (
B.5
), we have

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{s=1}^{t}\left(y_{s}(1)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=1]}{p_{s}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}+\eta_{t}^{-1}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}(1)\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle-\left[\sum_{s=1}^{t}\left(y_{s}(1)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=1]}{p_{s}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}(1)\rangle\right)+\eta_{t}^{-1}\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}(1)\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{Y}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}(\boldsymbol{I}_{t-1}-\boldsymbol{H})\boldsymbol{Y}_{t-1}+(y_{t}-\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{Y}_{t-1})^{2}-\boldsymbol{Y}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}(\boldsymbol{I}_{t-1}-\boldsymbol{H})\boldsymbol{Y}_{t-1}-\frac{1}{1+h}(y_{t}-\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{Y}_{t-1})^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{h}{1+h}(y_{t}-\boldsymbol{\alpha}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{Y}_{t-1})^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\boldsymbol{x}_{t}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}(\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}+\eta_{t}^{-1}\boldsymbol{I}_{d})^{-1}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}}{1+\boldsymbol{x}_{t}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}(\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}+\eta_{t}^{-1}\boldsymbol{I}_{d})^{-1}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}}\left(y_{t}(1)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{t}=1]}{p_{t}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\Pi_{t,t}}{1+\Pi_{t,t}}\left(y_{t}(1)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{t}=1]}{p_{t}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}\kern 5.0pt.

(25)

By the same method, we can also prove that

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{s=1}^{t}\left(y_{s}(0)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=0]}{1-p_{s}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}(0)\rangle\right)^{2}+\eta_{t}^{-1}\|{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}(0)\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle-\left[\sum_{s=1}^{t}\left(y_{s}(0)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=0]}{1-p_{s}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}(0)\rangle\right)+\eta_{t}^{-1}\|\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}(0)\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\Pi_{t,t}}{1+\Pi_{t,t}}\left(y_{t}(0)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{t}=0]}{1-p_{t}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(0)\rangle\right)^{2}\kern 5.0pt.

(26)

and

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{s=1}^{t}\left(y_{s}(1)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=1]}{p_{s}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}(1)\rangle\right)\cdot\left(y_{s}(0)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=0]}{1-p_{s}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}(0)\rangle\right)+\eta_{t}^{-1}\langle{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}(1),{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}(0)\rangle\right]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\left[\sum_{s=1}^{t}\left(y_{s}(1)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=1]}{p_{s}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}(1)\rangle\right)\cdot\left(y_{s}(0)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=0]}{1-p_{s}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}(1)\rangle\right)+\eta_{t}^{-1}\langle\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}(1),\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}(0)\rangle\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\Pi_{t,t}}{1+\Pi_{t,t}}\left(y_{t}(1)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{t}=1]}{p_{t}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)\rangle\right)\left(y_{t}(0)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{t}=0]}{1-p_{t}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(0)\rangle\right)\kern 5.0pt.

(27)

By (
B.5
), (
B.5
) and (
B.5
), we can finally prove that

\displaystyle\tilde{L}_{t+1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1),\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(0))-\tilde{L}_{t+1}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}(1),\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}(0))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{E}(0)}{\mathcal{E}(1)}\cdot\frac{\Pi_{t,t}}{1+\Pi_{t,t}}\left(y_{t}(1)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{t}=1]}{p_{t}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}+\frac{\mathcal{E}(1)}{\mathcal{E}(0)}\cdot\frac{\Pi_{t,t}}{1+\Pi_{t,t}}\left(y_{t}(0)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{t}=0]}{1-p_{t}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(0)\rangle\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+2\cdot\frac{\Pi_{t,t}}{1+\Pi_{t,t}}\left(y_{t}(1)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{t}=1]}{p_{t}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)\rangle\right)\left(y_{t}(0)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{t}=0]}{1-p_{t}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(0)\rangle\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\Pi_{t,t}}{1+\Pi_{t,t}}\widehat{\ell}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1),\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(0))\kern 5.0pt.

∎

Proposition
4.11
*
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
3
and Condition
1
, the expected prediction regret is bounded as

\displaystyle\Esym[\mathcal{R}_{T}^{\text{pred}}]\leq\left[(c_{1}^{2}c_{2}\max\{c_{2},1\})\left(\frac{c_{1}}{c_{0}}+\frac{c_{0}}{c_{1}}+2\right)+o(1)\right]T^{1/2}R^{2}\kern 5.0pt.

Proof.

By the definition of
\widehat{\ell}_{t}
, we have the following decomposition:

\displaystyle\widehat{\ell}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1),\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(0))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{E}(0)}{\mathcal{E}(1)}\left(y_{t}(1)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{t}=1]}{p_{t}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}+\frac{\mathcal{E}(1)}{\mathcal{E}(0)}\left(y_{t}(0)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{t}=0]}{1-p_{t}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(0)\rangle\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+2\left(y_{t}(1)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{t}=1]}{p_{t}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)\rangle\right)\left(y_{t}(0)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{t}=0]}{1-p_{t}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(0)\rangle\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{E}(0)}{\mathcal{E}(1)}\left(y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}+\frac{\mathcal{E}(1)}{\mathcal{E}(0)}\left(y_{t}(0)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(0)\rangle\right)^{2}+2\left(y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)\rangle\right)\left(y_{t}(0)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(0)\rangle\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathcal{E}(0)}{\mathcal{E}(1)}y_{t}^{2}(1)\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{t}=1]}{p_{t}}-1\right)^{2}+\frac{2\mathcal{E}(0)}{\mathcal{E}(1)}y_{t}(1)(y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)\rangle)\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{t}=1]}{p_{t}}-1\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathcal{E}(0)}{\mathcal{E}(1)}y_{t}^{2}(0)\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{t}=0]}{1-p_{t}}-1\right)^{2}+\frac{2\mathcal{E}(1)}{\mathcal{E}(0)}y_{t}(0)(y_{t}(0)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(0)\rangle)\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{t}=0]}{1-p_{t}}-1\right)

\displaystyle+2y_{t}(1)y_{t}(0)\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{t}=1]}{p_{t}}-1\right)\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{t}=0]}{1-p_{t}}-1\right)+2y_{t}(1)\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{t}=1]}{p_{t}}-1\right)(y_{t}(0)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(0)\rangle)

\displaystyle+2y_{t}(0)\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{t}=0]}{1-p_{t}}-1\right)(y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)\rangle)\kern 5.0pt.

It is easy to see that among all terms, term 5, 7, 9, 10 have mean zero by law of iterated expectation.
Hence by Lemma
4.10
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\tilde{L}_{t+1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1),\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(0))-\tilde{L}_{t+1}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}(1),\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}(0)))\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Pi_{t,t}\widehat{\ell}_{t}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1),\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(0))\right]~~~~\text{(Lemma \ref{lemma:ptpredloss-diff})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{E}(0)}{\mathcal{E}(1)}\mathrm{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Pi_{t,t}\left(y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}\right]+\frac{\mathcal{E}(1)}{\mathcal{E}(0)}\mathrm{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Pi_{t,t}\left(y_{t}(0)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(0)\rangle\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+2\mathrm{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Pi_{t,t}\left(y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)\rangle\right)\left(y_{t}(0)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(0)\rangle\right)\right]+\frac{\mathcal{E}(0)}{\mathcal{E}(1)}\mathrm{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Pi_{t,t}y_{t}^{2}(1)\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{t}=1]}{p_{t}}-1\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{\mathcal{E}(1)}{\mathcal{E}(0)}\mathrm{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Pi_{t,t}y_{t}^{2}(0)\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{t}=0]}{1-p_{t}}-1\right)^{2}\right]+2\mathrm{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Pi_{t,t}y_{t}(1)y_{t}(0)\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{t}=1]}{p_{t}}-1\right)\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{t}=0]}{1-p_{t}}-1\right)\right]

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle\frac{\mathcal{E}(0)}{\mathcal{E}(1)}S_{1}+\frac{\mathcal{E}(1)}{\mathcal{E}(0)}S_{2}+2S_{3}+\frac{\mathcal{E}(0)}{\mathcal{E}(1)}S_{4}+\frac{\mathcal{E}(1)}{\mathcal{E}(0)}S_{5}+2S_{6}\kern 5.0pt.

(28)

By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, Corollary
B.4
, Corollary
B.8
, Lemma
4.6
* and Assumption
3
, we have the following upper bound on
S_{1}
:

\displaystyle S_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Pi_{t,t}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\mathrm{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)\rangle\right)^{4}\right]\right)^{1/2}~~~~\text{(Cauchy-Schwarz inequality)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Pi_{t,t}^{2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\mathrm{E}\left[\eta_{T}^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\eta_{t}\left(y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)\rangle\right)^{4}\right]\right)^{1/2}~~~~\text{($\eta_{t}$ is decreasing)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}R_{t}^{4}((t-1)\vee\eta_{t}^{-1})^{-2}\right)^{1/2}R_{T}^{2}T^{1/2}~~~~\text{(Corollary \ref{coro4.1} and Lemma \ref{lemma:bounded-fourth-moments}*)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{T}^{-1}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}((t-1)R_{t}^{-2}\vee T^{1/2})^{-2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\eta_{T}^{-1}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}((t-1)R_{T}^{-2}\vee T^{1/2})^{-2}\right)^{1/2}~~~~\text{($R_{t}$ is increasing)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{T}^{-1}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}R_{T}^{4}((t-1)\vee\eta_{T}^{-1})^{-2}\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\eta_{T}^{-1}\cdot R_{T}^{2}\eta_{T}^{1/2}~~~~\text{(Lemma \ref{lem4.3})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{T}^{-1}\cdot(TR_{T}^{-4})^{-1/4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(\eta_{T}^{-1})~~~~\text{(Assumption \ref{assumption:maximum-radius})}\kern 5.0pt.

(29)

Similar, we can prove that

\displaystyle S_{2}=o(\eta_{T}^{-1}),\quad S_{3}=o(\eta_{T}^{-1})\kern 5.0pt.

(30)

By Lemma
B.9
, Corollary
4.15
, Corollary
B.17
, Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and Assumption
1
, we have

\displaystyle S_{4}=

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Pi_{t,t}y_{t}^{2}(1)\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{t}=1]}{p_{t}}-1\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Pi_{t,t}y_{t}^{2}(1)\mathrm{E}\left[\frac{1}{p_{t}}-1\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}R_{t}^{2}((t-1)\vee\eta_{t}^{-1})^{-1}y_{t}^{2}(1)\cdot\eta_{t}^{1/4}T^{1/4}\quad(\text{Corollary \ref{corollary:p-moment} and \ref{coro4.7}})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle T^{1/8}\sum_{t=1}^{T}R_{t}^{2}((t-1)\vee\eta_{t}^{-1})^{-1}y_{t}^{2}(1)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle T^{1/8}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}R_{t}^{4}((t-1)\vee\eta_{t}^{-1})^{-2}\right)^{1/2}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}y_{t}^{4}(1)\right)^{1/2}\quad\text{(Cauchy-Schwarz inequality)}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle T^{5/8}\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}R_{T}^{4}((t-1)\vee\eta_{T}^{-1})^{-2}\right)^{1/2}\text{(Assumption \ref{assumption:moments}})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle T^{5/8}R_{T}^{2}\eta_{T}^{1/2}\quad\text{(Lemma \ref{lem4.3})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{T}^{-1}\cdot T^{-1/8}R_{T}^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o(\eta_{T}^{-1})\kern 5.0pt.

(31)

Similar, we can prove that

\displaystyle S_{5}=o(\eta_{T}^{-1}),\quad S_{6}=o(\eta_{T}^{-1})\kern 5.0pt.

(32)

Hence by Assumption
1
, (
B.5
), (
B.5
), (
30
), (
B.5
) and (
32
), we have proved that

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}(\tilde{L}_{t+1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1),\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(0))-\tilde{L}_{t+1}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}(1),\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}(0)))\right]\lesssim S_{1}+S_{2}+S_{3}+S_{4}+S_{5}+S_{6}=o(\eta_{T}^{-1})\kern 5.0pt.

(33)

On the other hand, by Lemma
B.5
, Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, Assumption
1
and Assumption
2
, we have the following upper bound:

\displaystyle\eta_{T+1}^{-1}m(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}(1),\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}(0))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{T}^{-1}\left[\frac{\mathcal{E}(0)}{\mathcal{E}(1)}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}(1)\|^{2}+\frac{\mathcal{E}(1)}{\mathcal{E}(0)}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}(0)\|^{2}+2\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}(1),\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}(0)\rangle\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\eta_{T}^{-1}\left[\left(\frac{\mathcal{E}(0)}{\mathcal{E}(1)}+1\right)\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}(1)\|^{2}+\left(\frac{\mathcal{E}(1)}{\mathcal{E}(0)}+1\right)\|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}(0)\|^{2}\right]\quad\text{(Cauchy-Schwarz inequality)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{T}^{-1}\left[\left(\frac{\mathcal{E}(0)}{\mathcal{E}(1)}+1\right)\boldsymbol{Y}_{T}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}(1)\boldsymbol{X}_{T}\left(\boldsymbol{X}_{T}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{X}_{T}\right)^{-2}\boldsymbol{X}_{T}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{Y}_{T}(1)+\left(\frac{\mathcal{E}(1)}{\mathcal{E}(0)}+1\right)\boldsymbol{Y}_{T}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}(0)\boldsymbol{X}_{T}\left(\boldsymbol{X}_{T}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{X}_{T}\right)^{-2}\boldsymbol{X}_{T}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{Y}_{T}(0)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\eta_{T}^{-1}\left[\left(\frac{\mathcal{E}(0)}{\mathcal{E}(1)}+1\right)\|\boldsymbol{Y}_{T}(1)\|_{2}^{2}+\left(\frac{\mathcal{E}(1)}{\mathcal{E}(0)}+1\right)\|\boldsymbol{Y}_{T}(0)\|_{2}^{2}\right]\left\|\boldsymbol{X}_{T}\left(\boldsymbol{X}_{T}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{X}_{T}\right)^{-2}\boldsymbol{X}_{T}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\eta_{T}^{-1}T^{1/2}\left[\left(\frac{\mathcal{E}(0)}{\mathcal{E}(1)}+1\right)\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}y_{t}^{4}(1)\right)^{1/2}+\left(\frac{\mathcal{E}(1)}{\mathcal{E}(0)}+1\right)\left(\sum_{t=1}^{T}y_{t}^{4}(0)\right)^{1/2}\right]\left\|\boldsymbol{X}_{T}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{X}_{T}\left(\boldsymbol{X}_{T}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{X}_{T}\right)^{-2}\right\|_{2}~~\text{(Cauchy-Schwarz)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\eta_{T}^{-1}c_{1}^{2}T\left(\frac{\mathcal{E}(0)}{\mathcal{E}(1)}+\frac{\mathcal{E}(1)}{\mathcal{E}(0)}+2\right)\left\|\left(\boldsymbol{X}_{T}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{X}_{T}\right)^{-1}\right\|_{2}~~~~\text{(Assumption \ref{assumption:moments})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\eta_{T}^{-1}c_{1}^{2}\left(\frac{\mathcal{E}(0)}{\mathcal{E}(1)}+\frac{\mathcal{E}(1)}{\mathcal{E}(0)}+2\right)T\cdot\frac{c_{2}}{T}~~\text{(Assumption \ref{assumption:covariate-regularity})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{1}^{2}c_{2}\left(\frac{c_{1}}{c_{0}}+\frac{c_{0}}{c_{1}}+2\right)T^{1/2}R_{T}^{2}~~~~\text{(Assumption \ref{assumption:moments})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(c_{1}^{2}c_{2}\max\{c_{2},1\})\left(\frac{c_{1}}{c_{0}}+\frac{c_{0}}{c_{1}}+2\right)T^{1/2}R^{2}\quad(\text{Lemma \ref{lem4.2}})\kern 5.0pt.

(34)

Hence by Lemma
4.9
, (
33
) and (
B.5
), we have

\displaystyle\Esym[\mathcal{R}_{T}^{\text{pred}}]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{m(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}(1),\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}(0))}{\eta_{T+1}}+\mathrm{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\tilde{L}_{t+1}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1),\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(0))-\tilde{L}_{t+1}(\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}(1),\widetilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t+1}(0))\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left[(c_{1}^{2}c_{2}\max\{c_{2},1\})\left(\frac{c_{1}}{c_{0}}+\frac{c_{0}}{c_{1}}+2\right)+o(1)\right]T^{1/2}R^{2}\kern 5.0pt.

∎

B.6
Neyman Regret Analysis (Theorem
4.1
)

Based on the results proved in the previous sections, we can finally prove Theorem
4.1
.

Theorem
4.1
*
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
3
and Condition
1
, the Neyman Regret is bounded as

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{\textrm{Neyman}}_{T}\leq

\displaystyle\max\{c_{2},1\}\Bigg(\frac{8}{b_{3}^{3}}\left[\left(\frac{c_{1}}{c_{0}}-1\right)^{3}+\frac{b_{3}}{4}\left(\frac{c_{1}}{c_{0}}-1\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+b_{1}^{2}\max\{b_{1},2\}c_{1}^{4}\left(1+2^{-1/2}(\gamma_{0}\vee 4c_{2})^{1/2}\right)^{4}+c_{1}^{2}c_{2}\left(\frac{c_{1}}{c_{0}}+\frac{c_{0}}{c_{1}}+2\right)+o(1)\Bigg)T^{-1/2}R^{2}\kern 5.0pt.

Proof.

By Proposition
3.3
, Proposition
4.11
, Proposition
4.7
and Assumption
3
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}^{\textrm{Neyman}}_{T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\Esym[\mathcal{R}^{\mathrm{prob}}_{T}]+\frac{1}{T}\mathrm{E}[\mathcal{R}_{T}^{\text{pred}}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max\{c_{2},1\}\left(\frac{8}{b_{3}^{3}}\left[\left(\frac{c_{1}}{c_{0}}-1\right)^{3}+\frac{b_{3}}{4}\left(\frac{c_{1}}{c_{0}}-1\right)^{2}\right]+b_{1}^{2}\max\{b_{1},2\}c_{1}^{4}\left(1+2^{-1/2}(\gamma_{0}\vee 4c_{2})^{1/2}\right)^{4}+o(1)\right)T^{-1/2}R^{2}

\displaystyle+\left[(c_{1}^{2}c_{2}\max\{c_{2},1\})\left(\frac{c_{1}}{c_{0}}+\frac{c_{0}}{c_{1}}+2\right)+o(1)\right]T^{-1/2}R^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max\{c_{2},1\}\Bigg[\frac{8}{b_{3}^{3}}\left[\left(\frac{c_{1}}{c_{0}}-1\right)^{3}+\frac{b_{3}}{4}\left(\frac{c_{1}}{c_{0}}-1\right)^{2}\right]+b_{1}^{2}\max\{b_{1},2\}c_{1}^{4}\left(1+2^{-1/2}(\gamma_{0}\vee 4c_{2})^{1/2}\right)^{4}

\displaystyle+c_{1}^{2}c_{2}\left(\frac{c_{1}}{c_{0}}+\frac{c_{0}}{c_{1}}+2\right)\Bigg]T^{-1/2}R^{2}\kern 5.0pt.

∎

Appendix C
Inference Analysis

In this section, we aim to verify the inference procedure based on the Horvitz–Thompson estimator and the proposed variance estimator.
Section
C.1
establishes an almost sure upper bound for the inverse probabilities, which play a crucial role in verifying the central limit theorem.
Section
C.2
provides a detailed proof of the central limit theorem.
Section
C.3
demonstrates the equivalence between Assumption
4
and the well-known non-superefficiency condition in design-based causal inference.
Section
C.4
verifies the consistency of the proposed variance estimator.

C.1
Almost Sure Bounds on Inverse Probabilities

The following proposition derives the almost sure bounds for the inverse probabilties.

Proposition
5.4
.

Under Assumption
1
and Condition
1
, there exists a constant
K>0
so that

\Pr\Big\lparen\max\Big\{\frac{1}{p_{t}},\frac{1}{1-p_{t}}\Big\}\leq K\cdot T^{7/26}R_{t}^{-4/11}\ \text{ for all }t\in[T]\Big\rparen=1\kern 5.0pt.

Proof.

The proof contains three parts.
First, we obtain an initial uniform bound for
1/p_{t}
and
1/(1-p_{t})
. Then we refine the uniform bound in a sequential manner.
Finally, we derive an almost sure upper bound for each
t\in[T]
based on the obtained uniform bound.
Let
\mu_{1}>2
denote the largest solution to equation:
2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}\mu_{1}^{3/4}T^{1/8}=\mu_{1}
.
We then use induction method to prove that
\max_{t\in[T]}\left(\frac{1}{p_{t}}\vee\frac{1}{1-p_{t}}\right)\leq\mu_{1}
, which serves as the initial coarse uniform bound.
When
t=1
, we have
p_{t}=1/2
. Hence the result is proved.
If the result is proved for
1,\ldots,t
, now we derive the result for
t+1
.
Without loss of generality, we only derive the upper bound for
\widehat{A}_{t}(0)
.
By induction assumption and AM-GM inequality, we have

\displaystyle\widehat{A}_{t}(0)=

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{t}\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=0]}{1-p_{s}}\cdot\left(y_{s}(0)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s}(0)\rangle\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mu_{1}\sum_{s=1}^{t}\left(y_{s}(0)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s}(0)\rangle\right)^{2}~~~~(\text{induction assumption})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mu_{1}\sum_{s=1}^{t}\left[\left(y_{s}(0)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=0]}{1-p_{s}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s}(0)\rangle\right)-y_{s}(0)\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=0]}{1-p_{s}}-1\right)\right]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\mu_{1}\sum_{s=1}^{t}\left(y_{s}(0)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=0]}{1-p_{s}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s}(0)\rangle\right)^{2}+2\mu_{1}\sum_{s=1}^{t}y^{2}_{s}(0)\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=0]}{1-p_{s}}-1\right)^{2}~~~~(\text{AM-GM inequality})\kern 5.0pt.

We introduce the following notations:

\displaystyle\widehat{\ell}_{t}^{(0)}(\boldsymbol{\beta})

\displaystyle=\left(y_{t}(0)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{t}=0]}{1-p_{t}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}\rangle\right)^{2}\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle\widehat{G}_{t}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\beta})

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}\widehat{\ell}_{s}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\beta})+\eta_{t}^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\kern 5.0pt.

\displaystyle\bar{G}_{t}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\beta})

\displaystyle=\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}\widehat{\ell}_{s}^{(k)}(\boldsymbol{\beta})+\eta_{t-1}^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\kern 5.0pt.

Denote
\bar{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{t}(0)
be the minimizer of
\bar{G}_{t}^{(0)}(\boldsymbol{\beta})
.
By similar methods as in the proofs of Lemma
4.9
and Lemma
4.10
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{t}\left(y_{s}(0)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=0]}{1-p_{s}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s}(0)\rangle\right)^{2}-\sum_{s=1}^{t}\left(y_{s}(0)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=0]}{1-p_{s}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t+1}(0)\rangle\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{t}\widehat{\ell}_{s}^{(0)}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s}(0))-\sum_{s=1}^{t}\widehat{\ell}_{s}^{(0)}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t+1}(0))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\eta_{t+1}^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t+1}(0)\|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{s=1}^{t}(\bar{G}_{s+1}^{(0)}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s}(0))-\bar{G}_{s+1}^{(0)}(\bar{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{s+1}(0)))\quad(\text{by similar method as in Lemma \ref{lemma:standard-oco-for-pred-regret}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{t+1}^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t+1}(0)\|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{s=1}^{t}\frac{\Pi_{s,s}}{1+\Pi_{s,s}}\left(y_{s}(0)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=0]}{1-p_{s}}-\langle x_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s}(0)\rangle\right)^{2}\quad(\text{by similar proof as in Lemma \ref{lemma:ptpredloss-diff}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\eta_{t+1}^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t+1}(0)\|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{s=1}^{t}\boldsymbol{x}_{s}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\left(\boldsymbol{X}_{s-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{X}_{s-1}+\eta_{s}^{-1}\boldsymbol{I}_{d}\right)^{-1}\boldsymbol{x}_{s}\left(y_{s}(0)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=0]}{1-p_{s}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s}(0)\rangle\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\eta_{t+1}^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t+1}(0)\|_{2}^{2}+\sum_{s=1}^{t}R_{s}^{2}\eta_{s}\left(y_{s}(0)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=0]}{1-p_{s}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s}(0)\rangle\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\eta_{t+1}^{-1}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t+1}(0)\|_{2}^{2}+T^{-1/2}\sum_{s=1}^{t}\left(y_{s}(0)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=0]}{1-p_{s}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s}(0)\rangle\right)^{2}.

(35)

By induction assumption, the definition of
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t+1}(0)
and Assumption
1
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{t}\left(y_{s}(0)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=0]}{1-p_{s}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t+1}(0)\rangle\right)^{2}+\eta_{t+1}^{-1}\left\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t+1}(0)\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\boldsymbol{\beta}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sum_{s=1}^{t}\left(y_{s}(0)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=0]}{1-p_{s}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}\rangle\right)^{2}+\eta_{t+1}^{-1}\left\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{t}\left(y_{s}(0)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=0]}{1-p_{s}}\right)^{2}~~~~(\text{optimality of $\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t+1}(0)$})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{s=1}^{t}y_{s}^{2}(0)\right)\left(\max_{s=1,\ldots,t}\frac{1}{1-p_{s}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle c_{1}^{2}\mu_{1}^{2}T~~~~(\text{induction assumption, Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and Assumption \ref{assumption:moments}})\kern 5.0pt.

(36)

Note that
T^{-1/2}
should be smaller than
1/2
for
T
large enough.
Hence by (
C.1
) and (
C.1
), we have

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{t}\left(y_{s}(0)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=0]}{1-p_{s}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s}(0)\rangle\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-T^{-1/2}}\left(\sum_{s=1}^{t}\left(y_{s}(0)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=0]}{1-p_{s}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t+1}(0)\rangle\right)^{2}+\eta_{t+1}^{-1}\left\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t+1}(0)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\left(\sum_{s=1}^{t}\left(y_{s}(0)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=0]}{1-p_{s}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t+1}(0)\rangle\right)^{2}+\eta_{t+1}^{-1}\left\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t+1}(0)\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2c_{1}^{2}\mu_{1}^{2}T\kern 5.0pt.

(37)

By induction assumption and Assumption
1
, we also have

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{t}y^{2}_{s}(0)\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=0]}{1-p_{s}}-1\right)^{2}\leq\mu_{1}^{2}\sum_{s=1}^{t}y^{2}_{s}(0)\leq c_{1}^{2}\mu_{1}^{2}T\kern 5.0pt.

(38)

Hence by (
C.1
), (
38
), we have

\displaystyle\widehat{A}_{t}(0)\leq

\displaystyle 2\mu_{1}\sum_{s=1}^{t}\left(y_{s}(0)\cdot\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=0]}{1-p_{s}}-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s}(0)\rangle\right)^{2}+2\mu_{1}\sum_{s=1}^{t}y^{2}_{s}(0)\left[\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=0]}{1-p_{s}}-1\right]^{2}\leq 6c_{1}^{2}\mu_{1}^{3}T\kern 5.0pt.

Then by Lemma
4.14
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p_{t+1}}\leq

\displaystyle 2+b_{1}(b_{2}/6)^{1/4}\eta_{t+1}^{1/4}\widehat{A}^{1/4}_{t}(0)\leq 2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}\eta_{1}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}\mu_{1}^{3/4}T^{1/4}=2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}\mu_{1}^{3/4}T^{1/8}=\mu_{1}\kern 5.0pt.

Similarly we can prove that
\frac{1}{1-p_{t+1}}
is bounded by
\mu_{1}
. Then by induction method, we have

\displaystyle\max_{t\in[T]}\left(\frac{1}{p_{t}}\vee\frac{1}{1-p_{t}}\right)\leq\mu_{1}\kern 5.0pt.

Now we refine the initial uniform bound
\mu_{1}
in sequential steps.
Suppose the upper bound obtained in the
r
-th step is
\mu_{r}
, then we refine this bound to
\mu_{r+1}
in
r+1
-th step through the following procedure.
By similar steps, we can prove that
\widehat{A}_{T}(1)\leq 6c_{1}^{2}\mu_{r}^{3}T
and
\widehat{A}_{T}(0)\leq 6c_{1}^{2}\mu_{r}^{3}T
.
We fix a
T
-dependent constant
\delta>1
(determined later).
For any
t\in[T]
, if
\widehat{A}_{t-1}(1)\geq 6c_{1}^{2}\mu_{r}^{3}T\delta^{-2}
, then by Lemma
4.14
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p_{t}}\leq 2+b_{1}(b_{2}/b_{3})^{1/2}\left(\frac{\widehat{A}_{t-1}(0)}{\widehat{A}_{t-1}(1)}\right)^{1/2}\leq 2+b_{1}(b_{2}/b_{3})^{1/2}\left(\frac{6c_{1}^{2}\mu_{r}^{3}T}{6c_{1}^{2}\mu_{r}^{3}T\delta^{-2}}\right)^{1/2}=2+b_{1}(b_{2}/b_{3})^{1/2}\delta\kern 5.0pt.

If
\widehat{A}_{t-1}(1)\leq 6c_{1}^{2}\mu_{r}^{3}T\delta^{-2}
, then by Lemma
4.14
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p_{t}}\leq

\displaystyle 2+b_{1}(b_{2}/6)^{1/4}\eta_{t}^{1/4}\widehat{A}_{t-1}^{1/4}(0)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2+b_{1}(b_{2}/6)^{1/4}\eta_{t}^{1/4}\cdot\left(6c_{1}^{2}T\cdot\left[\max_{s=1,\ldots,t-1}\frac{1}{1-p_{s}}\right]^{3}\right)^{1/4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}\eta_{1}^{1/4}T^{1/4}\max_{s=1,\ldots,t-1}\left(2+b_{1}(b_{2}/6)^{1/4}\eta_{s}^{1/4}\widehat{A}^{1/4}_{s-1}(1)\right)^{3/4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}\eta_{1}^{1/4}T^{1/4}\left(2+b_{1}(b_{2}/6)^{1/4}\eta_{1}^{1/4}\widehat{A}^{1/4}_{t-1}(1)\right)^{3/4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}\eta_{1}^{1/4}T^{1/4}\left[2+b_{1}(b_{2}/6)^{1/4}\eta_{1}^{1/4}\left(6c_{1}^{2}\mu_{r}^{3}T\delta^{-2}\right)^{1/4}\right]^{3/4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}\eta_{1}^{1/4}T^{1/4}\left(2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}\eta_{1}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}\mu_{r}^{3/4}T^{1/4}\delta^{-1/2}\right)^{3/4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}T^{1/8}\left(2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}\mu_{r}^{3/4}T^{1/8}\delta^{-1/2}\right)^{3/4}\kern 5.0pt.

Now we choose
\delta>0
to be the largest solution to equation:

\displaystyle 2+b_{1}(b_{2}/b_{3})^{1/2}\delta=2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}T^{1/8}\left(2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}\mu_{r}^{3/4}T^{1/8}\delta^{-1/2}\right)^{3/4}\kern 5.0pt.

(39)

We first prove the existence of such
\delta
.
The left hand side of equation (
39
) attains value
2
at
\delta=0
and tends to
+\infty
when
\delta\rightarrow\infty
.
The right hand side of equation (
39
) tends to infinity when
\delta\downarrow 0
and tends to 2 when
\delta\rightarrow\infty
.
Hence the solution to (
39
) exists by intermediate value theorem for continuous functions and such
\delta
is well defined due to the continuity on both sides.
Then for any
t\in[T]
, we have
1/p_{t}\leq 2+b_{1}(b_{2}/b_{3})^{1/2}\delta\triangleq\mu_{r+1}
.
Similarly, we can prove that for any
t\in[T]
, we have
1/(1-p_{t})\leq 2+b_{1}(b_{2}/b_{3})^{1/2}\delta=\mu_{r+1}
.
Hence we obtain a new uniform bound
\mu_{r+1}
for
1/p_{t}
and
1/(1-p_{t})
.
By such sequential steps, we obtain a sequence of upper bounds:
\{\mu_{r}:r=1,2,\ldots\}
.
Suppose
\mu>2
is the largest solution to the following equation:

\displaystyle\mu=2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}T^{1/8}\left(2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}\mu^{3/4}T^{1/8}(b_{1}^{-1}(b_{2}/b_{3})^{-1/2}(\mu-2))^{-1/2}\right)^{3/4}\kern 5.0pt.

(40)

We first prove that existence of such
\mu
.
The left hand side of (
40
) attains value
2
at
\mu=2
, while the right hand side tends to infinity when
\mu\downarrow 2
.
Meanwhile, for any fixed
T
, the right hand side is of order
\mathcal{O}\lparen\mu^{3/16}\rparen
while the left hand side is or order
\mathcal{O}\lparen\mu\rparen
.
Hence by intermediate value theorem for continuous functions, there exists solution to (
40
) on
(2,\infty)
for any given
T
.
Hence
\mu
is well-defined due to the continuity on both sides. Then we have

\displaystyle\mu=

\displaystyle 2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}T^{1/8}\left(2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}\mu^{3/4}T^{1/8}(b_{1}^{-1}(b_{2}/b_{3})^{-1/2}(\mu-2))^{-1/2}\right)^{3/4}

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle 1+T^{1/8}\left[1+(\mu^{1/4}T^{1/8})^{3/4}\right]

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle 1+T^{1/8}\left(1+T^{3/32}\mu^{3/16}\right)

\displaystyle\asymp

\displaystyle T^{7/32}\mu^{3/16}\kern 5.0pt.

It is easy to see that
\mu_{1}=\Theta(T^{1/2})
and
\mu=\mathcal{O}\lparen T^{7/26}\rparen
, which indicates that
\mu=o(\mu_{1})
.
Hence for
T
large enough, we can assume WLOG that
\mu_{1}>\mu
.
Now we prove that the sequence
\{\mu_{r}:r=1,2,\ldots\}
is monotone decreasing and
\mu_{r}>\mu
for any
r
by induction method.
When
r=1
, the result is proved. Suppose the result is proved for
1,\ldots,r
, by the definition of
\mu_{r+1}
, there holds:

\displaystyle\mu_{r+1}=

\displaystyle 2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}T^{1/8}\left(2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}\mu_{r}^{3/4}T^{1/8}(b_{1}^{-1}(b_{2}/b_{3})^{-1/2}(\mu_{r+1}-2))^{-1/2}\right)^{3/4}\kern 5.0pt.\quad

(41)

Since
\mu>2
is the largest solution to equation (
40
), then either there holds

\displaystyle\delta<2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}T^{1/8}\left(2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}\delta^{3/4}T^{1/8}(b_{1}^{-1}(b_{2}/b_{3})^{-1/2}(\delta-2))^{-1/2}\right)^{3/4}

for any
\delta>\mu
or there holds

\displaystyle\delta>2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}T^{1/8}\left(2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}\delta^{3/4}T^{1/8}(b_{1}^{-1}(b_{2}/b_{3})^{-1/2}(\delta-2))^{-1/2}\right)^{3/4}

for any
\delta>\mu
due to the continuity on both sides.
For any fixed
T
, the left hand side has order
\mathcal{O}\lparen\delta\rparen
while the right hand side has order
\mathcal{O}\lparen\delta^{3/16}\rparen
, which indicates that the only possibility is that the second case holds.
Since
\mu_{r}>\mu
is proved by induction assumption, we have

\displaystyle\mu_{r}>2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}T^{1/8}\left(2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}\mu_{r}^{3/4}T^{1/8}(b_{1}^{-1}(b_{2}/b_{3})^{-1/2}(\mu_{r}-2))^{-1/2}\right)^{3/4}\kern 5.0pt.

Since the left hand side of (
39
) is monotone increasing while the right hand side is monotone decreasing, this together with (
41
) imply that
\mu_{r+1}<\mu_{r}
.
Then by induction method, we prove that
\{\mu_{r}\}
is monotone decreasing and
\mu_{r}>\mu
for any
r
.
Hence the limit of the sequence exists, which should also be a solution to (
40
) by continuity.
Since
\mu>2
is the largest solution of equation (
40
) by definition, it is easy to see that
\lim_{r\rightarrow\infty}\mu_{r}=\mu
.
Hence we can prove that

\displaystyle\max_{t\in[T]}\left(\frac{1}{p_{t}}\vee\frac{1}{1-p_{t}}\right)\leq\mu=\mathcal{O}\lparen T^{7/26}\rparen\kern 5.0pt.

Finally, we obtain the upper bound for each individual
t\in[T]
.
If
\widehat{A}_{t-1}(1)\geq 6c_{1}^{2}\mu^{3}T\delta^{-2}
, by Lemma
4.14
we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p_{t}}\leq 2+b_{1}(b_{2}/b_{3})^{1/2}\left(\frac{\widehat{A}_{t-1}(0)}{\widehat{A}_{t-1}(1)}\right)^{1/2}\leq 2+b_{1}(b_{2}/b_{3})^{1/2}\left(\frac{6c_{1}^{2}\mu^{3}T}{6c_{1}^{2}\mu^{3}T\delta^{-2}}\right)^{1/2}=2+b_{1}(b_{2}/b_{3})^{1/2}\delta\kern 5.0pt.

If
\widehat{A}_{t-1}(1)\leq 6c_{1}^{2}\mu^{3}T\delta^{-2}
, by Lemma
4.14
we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p_{t}}\leq

\displaystyle 2+b_{1}(b_{2}/6)^{1/4}\eta_{t}^{1/4}\widehat{A}_{t-1}^{1/4}(0)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2+b_{1}(b_{2}/6)^{1/4}\eta_{t}^{1/4}\cdot\left(6c_{1}^{2}T\cdot\left[\max_{s=1,\ldots,t-1}\frac{1}{1-p_{s}}\right]^{3}\right)^{1/4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}\eta_{t}^{1/4}T^{1/4}\max_{s=1,\ldots,t-1}\left(2+b_{1}(b_{2}/6)^{1/4}\eta_{s}^{1/4}\widehat{A}^{1/4}_{s-1}(1)\right)^{3/4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}\eta_{t}^{1/4}T^{1/4}\left(2+b_{1}(b_{2}/6)^{1/4}\eta_{1}^{1/4}\widehat{A}^{1/4}_{t-1}(1)\right)^{3/4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}\eta_{t}^{1/4}T^{1/4}\left[2+b_{1}(b_{2}/6)^{1/4}\eta_{1}^{1/4}\left(6c_{1}^{2}\mu^{3}T\delta^{-2}\right)^{1/4}\right]^{3/4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}\eta_{t}^{1/4}T^{1/4}\left(2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}\mu^{3/4}T^{1/8}\delta^{-1/2}\right)^{3/4}\kern 5.0pt.

Let
\delta_{t}>0
be the largest solution to equation:

\displaystyle 2+b_{1}(b_{2}/b_{3})^{1/2}\delta=2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}\eta_{t}^{1/4}T^{1/4}\left(2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}\mu^{3/4}T^{1/8}\delta^{-1/2}\right)^{3/4}\kern 5.0pt.

(42)

Choose
\delta=(2(b_{2}/b_{3})^{-1/2}b_{1}^{3/4}b_{2}^{7/16}c_{1}^{7/8}\eta_{t}^{1/4}T^{11/32}\mu^{9/16})^{8/11}
, for
T
large enough it is easy to see that

\displaystyle b_{1}(b_{2}/b_{3})^{1/2}\delta-b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}\eta_{t}^{1/4}T^{1/4}\left(2+b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}\mu^{3/4}T^{1/8}\delta^{-1/2}\right)^{3/4}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle b_{1}(b_{2}/b_{3})^{1/2}\delta-2b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}\eta_{t}^{1/4}T^{1/4}\left(b_{1}b_{2}^{1/4}c_{1}^{1/2}\mu^{3/4}T^{1/8}\delta^{-1/2}\right)^{3/4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle b_{1}(b_{2}/b_{3})^{1/2}\delta-2b_{1}^{7/4}b_{2}^{7/16}c_{1}^{7/8}\eta_{t}^{1/4}T^{11/32}\mu^{9/16}\delta^{-3/8}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0\kern 5.0pt.

Since the left hand side of (
42
) is monotone increasing in
\delta
while the right hand side is monotone decreasing in
\delta
, this implies that

\displaystyle\max\left\{\frac{1}{p_{t}},\frac{1}{1-p_{t}}\right\}\leq\delta_{t}\leq\delta=

\displaystyle(2(b_{2}/b_{3})^{-1/2}b_{2}^{7/16}c_{1}^{7/8}\eta_{t}^{1/4}T^{11/32}\mu^{9/16})^{8/11}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2(b_{2}/b_{3})^{-1/2}b_{2}^{7/16}c_{1}^{7/8}T^{7/32}R_{t}^{-1/2}\mu^{9/16})^{8/11}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(2(b_{2}/b_{3})^{-1/2}b_{2}^{7/16}c_{1}^{7/8})^{8/11}T^{7/44}\mu^{9/22}R_{t}^{-4/11}\kern 5.0pt.

Since
\mu=\mathcal{O}\lparen T^{7/26}\rparen
, we have shown that there exists a constant
K>0
(independent of
t
and
T
) such that

\displaystyle\Pr\Big\lparen\max\Big\{\frac{1}{p_{t}},\frac{1}{1-p_{t}}\Big\}\leq K\cdot T^{7/26}R_{t}^{-4/11}\ \text{ for all }t\in[T]\Big\rparen=1\kern 5.0pt.

∎

C.2
Central Limit Theorem

In this section, we prove the central limit theorem for the Horvitz-Thompson estimator.
We begin by stating the martingale central limit theorem, which serves as the main tool for our proof.

Lemma
5.3

(
12
)
.

If
X_{t,T}
is a triangular array of martingale difference sequences with respect to filtrations
\mathcal{F}_{t,T}
, i.e.
\Esym[X_{t,T}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1,T}]=0
, then if

1.

There exists
\delta>0
such that
\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Esym[X_{t,T}^{2+\delta}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1,T}]\xrightarrow{p}0

2.

V_{T}^{2}\triangleq\sum_{t=1}^{T}\Esym[X_{t,T}^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t-1,T}]\xrightarrow{p}1

then the sum
S_{T}=\sum_{t=1}^{T}X_{t,T}
converges to a standard normal in distribution,
S_{T}\xrightarrow{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
.

In Section 5, we have already defined the martingale difference sequence:

\displaystyle X_{t,T}=

\displaystyle\frac{\hat{\tau}_{t}-\tau_{t}}{T\sqrt{\Varsym\lparen\hat{\tau}\rparen}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T\sqrt{\Varsym\lparen\hat{\tau}\rparen}}\Bigg[\big\lparen y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)\rangle\big\rparen\cdot\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{t}=1]}{p_{t}}-1\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad-\big\lparen y_{t}(0)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(0)\rangle\big\rparen\cdot\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{t}=0]}{1-p_{t}}-1\right)\Bigg]\kern 5.0pt.

In the following two subsections, we separately verify that the martingale difference sequence
\{X_{t,T}\}
satisfy the two conditions in Lemma
5.3
.
We first state, without proof, the non-superefficiency condition in the following corollary. Its detailed proof is deferred to Section
C.3
.

Corollary
5.2

(Non-Superefficiency)
.

Under Assumptions
1
-
4
and Condition
1
,
\liminf_{T\to\infty}T\cdot\Varsym\lparen\hat{\tau}\rparen>0
.

C.2.1
Stable Variance Condition

We first derive a simplified form of the stable variance condition.
From the expression of
\operatorname{Var}(\hat{\tau})
, the term
V_{T}^{2}
can be simplified as:

\displaystyle V_{T}^{2}=

\displaystyle T^{-2}\operatorname{Var}^{-1}(\hat{\tau})\Bigg[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)\rangle\right\}^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{p_{t}}-1\right)+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{y_{t}(0)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(0)\rangle\right\}^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{1-p_{t}}-1\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+2\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)\rangle\right\}\cdot\left\{y_{t}(0)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(0)\rangle\right\}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1+T^{-2}\operatorname{Var}^{-1}(\hat{\tau})\Bigg[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)\rangle\right\}^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{p_{t}}-1\right)+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{y_{t}(0)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(0)\rangle\right\}^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{1-p_{t}}-1\right)

\displaystyle-\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{E}\left(\left\{y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)\rangle\right\}^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{p_{t}}-1\right)\right)-\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{E}\left(\left(y_{t}(0)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(0)\rangle\right)^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{1-p_{t}}-1\right)\right)

\displaystyle+2\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)\rangle\right\}\left\{y_{t}(0)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(0)\rangle\right\}-2\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathrm{E}\left(\{y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)\rangle\}\{y_{t}(0)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(0)\rangle\}\right)\Bigg]\kern 5.0pt.

Under the non-superefficiency condition (Corollary
5.2
), to prove that
V_{T}^{2}\xrightarrow{p}1
, it suffices to establish the following three convergence results:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)\rangle\right\}^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{p_{t}}-1\right)-\mathrm{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)\rangle\right\}^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{p_{t}}-1\right)\right]\right]\xrightarrow{p}0\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{y_{t}(0)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(0)\rangle\right\}^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{1-p_{t}}-1\right)-\mathrm{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{y_{t}(0)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(0)\rangle\right\}^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{1-p_{t}}-1\right)\right]\right]\xrightarrow{p}0\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)\rangle\right\}\left\{y_{t}(0)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(0)\rangle\right\}-\mathrm{E}\left[\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left\{y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)\rangle\right\}\left\{y_{t}(0)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(0)\rangle\right\}\right]\right]\xrightarrow{p}0\kern 5.0pt.

(43)

We begin with the first convergence result in (
C.2.1
). By direct calculation, we obtain the following decomposition:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{p_{t}}-1\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}^{*}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{p_{t}}-1\right)+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left[\left(y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}-\left(y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}^{*}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}\right]\left(\frac{1}{p_{t}}-1\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}^{*}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{p_{t}}-\frac{1}{\bar{p}_{t}}\right)+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}^{*}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{\bar{p}_{t}}-1\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left[2(y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}^{*}(1)\rangle)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}_{t}(1)\rangle\right]\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}_{t}(1)\rangle\left(\frac{1}{p_{t}}-1\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}^{*}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{p_{t}}-\frac{1}{\bar{p}_{t}}\right)+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}^{*}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{\bar{p}_{t}}-1\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}^{*}(1)\rangle)\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}_{t}(1)\rangle\left(\frac{1}{p_{t}}-\frac{1}{\bar{p}_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}^{*}(1)\rangle)\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}_{t}(1)\rangle\left(\frac{1}{\bar{p}_{t}}-1\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}_{t}(1)\rangle^{2}\left(\frac{1}{p_{t}}-\frac{1}{\bar{p}_{t}}\right)+\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}_{t}(1)\rangle^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\bar{p}_{t}}-1\right)\kern 5.0pt.

Hence, it suffices to show that each term concentrates around its respective expectation, i.e.,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}^{*}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{p_{t}}-\frac{1}{\bar{p}_{t}}\right)-\mathrm{E}\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}^{*}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{p_{t}}-\frac{1}{\bar{p}_{t}}\right)\right]\xrightarrow{p}0\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}^{*}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{\bar{p}_{t}}-1\right)-\mathrm{E}\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}^{*}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}\cdot\left(\frac{1}{\bar{p}_{t}}-1\right)\right]\xrightarrow{p}0\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}^{*}(1)\rangle)\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}_{t}(1)\rangle\left(\frac{1}{p_{t}}-\frac{1}{\bar{p}_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle-\mathrm{E}\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}^{*}(1)\rangle)\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}_{t}(1)\rangle\left(\frac{1}{p_{t}}-\frac{1}{\bar{p}_{t}}\right)\right]\xrightarrow{p}0\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}^{*}(1)\rangle)\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}_{t}(1)\rangle\left(\frac{1}{\bar{p}_{t}}-1\right)

\displaystyle-\mathrm{E}\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}^{*}(1)\rangle)\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}_{t}(1)\rangle\left(\frac{1}{\bar{p}_{t}}-1\right)\right]\xrightarrow{p}0\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}_{t}(1)\rangle^{2}\left(\frac{1}{p_{t}}-\frac{1}{\bar{p}_{t}}\right)-\mathrm{E}\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}_{t}(1)\rangle^{2}\left(\frac{1}{p_{t}}-\frac{1}{\bar{p}_{t}}\right)\right]\xrightarrow{p}0\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}_{t}(1)\rangle^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\bar{p}_{t}}-1\right)-\mathrm{E}\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}_{t}(1)\rangle^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\bar{p}_{t}}-1\right)\right]\xrightarrow{p}0\kern 5.0pt.

(44)

The second convergence result in (
C.2.1
) automatically holds since no randomness is involved.
Under Markov’s inequality, we only need to prove the following convergence results:

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}^{*}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}\cdot\left|\frac{1}{p_{t}}-\frac{1}{\bar{p}_{t}}\right|\right]\rightarrow 0\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}^{*}(1)\rangle)\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}_{t}(1)\rangle\left|\frac{1}{p_{t}}-\frac{1}{\bar{p}_{t}}\right|\right]\rightarrow 0\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(y_{t}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}^{*}(1)\rangle)\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}_{t}(1)\rangle\left|\frac{1}{\bar{p}_{t}}-1\right|\right]\rightarrow 0\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}_{t}(1)\rangle^{2}\left|\frac{1}{p_{t}}-\frac{1}{\bar{p}_{t}}\right|\right]\rightarrow 0\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}\left[\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{t}(1)-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}_{t}(1)\rangle^{2}\left|\frac{1}{\bar{p}_{t}}-1\right|\right]\rightarrow 0\kern 5.0pt.

(45)

Before proving the first convergence result in (
C.2.1
), we establish several supporting lemmas that control the difference between the inverse probabilities associated with
p_{t}
and
\bar{p}_{t}
.
The following two lemmas bound the difference between the inverse probabilities in terms of the differences between the corresponding first-order equations.
Lemma
C.1
discusses the two cases where both probabilities are greater than
1/2
or both are less than
1/2
.

Lemma C.1
.

Let
A,\tilde{A},B,\tilde{B}
be positive constants.
Suppose
p,\tilde{p}
satisfy

\displaystyle-\frac{A}{p^{2}}+\frac{B}{(1-p)^{2}}+\Psi^{\prime}(p)

\displaystyle=0\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle-\frac{\tilde{A}}{\tilde{p}^{2}}+\frac{\tilde{B}}{(1-\tilde{p})^{2}}+\Psi^{\prime}(\tilde{p})

\displaystyle=0\kern 5.0pt.

(1)

If
A\geq B,\tilde{A}\geq\tilde{B}
, under Condition
1
we can attain the following upper bound:

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{1-p}-\frac{1}{1-\tilde{p}}\right|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{b_{1}b_{2}}{b_{3}}\left(1+\frac{2}{b_{3}}\right)\frac{|A-\tilde{A}|}{A}\frac{p}{1-p}+\frac{2b_{1}^{2}b_{2}}{b_{3}^{2}}\left(1+\frac{2}{b_{3}}\right)\frac{|B-\tilde{B}|}{B}\frac{\tilde{p}}{1-\tilde{p}}\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}\right|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{b_{2}^{2}}{2b_{3}}\frac{|A-\tilde{A}|}{A}+\frac{b_{1}b_{2}}{b_{3}}\left(1+\frac{2}{b_{3}}\right)\frac{|B-\tilde{B}|}{A}\frac{p}{1-p}\kern 5.0pt.

(2)

If
A\leq B,\tilde{A}\leq\tilde{B}
, under Condition
1
we can attain the following upper bound:

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}\right|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{b_{1}b_{2}}{b_{3}}\left(1+\frac{2}{b_{3}}\right)\frac{|B-\tilde{B}|}{B}\frac{1-p}{p}+\frac{2b_{1}^{2}b_{2}}{b_{3}^{2}}\left(1+\frac{2}{b_{3}}\right)\frac{|A-\tilde{A}|}{A}\frac{1-\tilde{p}}{\tilde{p}}\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{1-p}-\frac{1}{1-\tilde{p}}\right|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{b_{2}^{2}}{2b_{3}}\frac{|B-\tilde{B}|}{B}+\frac{b_{1}b_{2}}{b_{3}}\left(1+\frac{2}{b_{3}}\right)\frac{|A-\tilde{A}|}{B}\frac{1-p}{p}\kern 5.0pt.

Proof.

(1)

For
u=\phi^{-1}(p)
and
\tilde{u}=\phi^{-1}(\tilde{p})
, it is easy to see that
u
and
\tilde{u}
satisfy

\displaystyle A\left(\frac{1}{\phi(u)}\right)^{\prime}+B\left(\frac{1}{1-\phi(u)}\right)^{\prime}+u+3u|u|=

\displaystyle 0\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle\tilde{A}\left(\frac{1}{\phi(\tilde{u})}\right)^{\prime}+\tilde{B}\left(\frac{1}{1-\phi(\tilde{u})}\right)^{\prime}+\tilde{u}+3\tilde{u}|\tilde{u}|=

\displaystyle 0\kern 5.0pt.

(46)

Since
A\geq B,\tilde{A}\geq\tilde{B}
, it is easy to see that
u,\tilde{u}\geq 0
.
By subtracting the second equation in (
(1)
) from the first equation, we have

\displaystyle A\left[\left(\frac{1}{\phi(u)}\right)^{\prime}-\left(\frac{1}{\phi(\tilde{u})}\right)^{\prime}\right]+(A-\tilde{A})\left(\frac{1}{\phi(\tilde{u})}\right)^{\prime}+B\left[\left(\frac{1}{1-\phi(u)}\right)^{\prime}-\left(\frac{1}{1-\phi(\tilde{u})}\right)^{\prime}\right]

\displaystyle+(B-\tilde{B})\left(\frac{1}{1-\phi(\tilde{u})}\right)^{\prime}+(u-\tilde{u})(1+3u+3\tilde{u})=0\kern 5.0pt.

(47)

By the convexity assumption in Condition
1
,
A\left[\left(\frac{1}{\phi(u)}\right)^{\prime}-\left(\frac{1}{\phi(\tilde{u})}\right)^{\prime}\right]
,
B\left[\left(\frac{1}{1-\phi(u)}\right)^{\prime}-\left(\frac{1}{1-\phi(\tilde{u})}\right)^{\prime}\right]
and
(u-\tilde{u})(1+3u+3\tilde{u})
always have the same sign. Hence by Lemma
B.16
, we have

\displaystyle\left|A\left[\left(\frac{1}{\phi(u)}\right)^{\prime}-\left(\frac{1}{\phi(\tilde{u})}\right)^{\prime}\right]+B\left[\left(\frac{1}{1-\phi(u)}\right)^{\prime}-\left(\frac{1}{1-\phi(\tilde{u})}\right)^{\prime}\right]+\eta^{-1}(u-\tilde{u})(1+3u+3\tilde{u})\right|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle A\left|\left(\frac{1}{\phi(u)}\right)^{\prime}-\left(\frac{1}{\phi(\tilde{u})}\right)^{\prime}\right|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{b_{3}}{2}\frac{A}{(1+\tilde{u})(1+u)(1+\tilde{u}\land u)}\cdot|u-\tilde{u}|\kern 5.0pt.

(48)

By monotonicty assumption in Condition
1
and (
(1)
), we also have

\displaystyle A=\frac{B\left(\frac{1}{1-\phi(u)}\right)^{\prime}+u+3u^{2}}{\left(\frac{1}{\phi(u)}\right)^{\prime}}\geq B\frac{\left(\frac{1}{1-\phi(u)}\right)^{\prime}}{\left(\frac{1}{\phi(u)}\right)^{\prime}}\kern 5.0pt.

(49)

Hence by (
(1)
), (
(1)
), (
49
), Condition
1
and Lemma
B.16
, we have

\displaystyle|u-\tilde{u}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{b_{3}}{2}\frac{A}{(1+\tilde{u})(1+u)(1+\tilde{u}\land u)}\right)^{-1}\left|(A-\tilde{A})\left(\frac{1}{\phi(\tilde{u})}\right)^{\prime}+(B-\tilde{B})\left(\frac{1}{1-\phi(\tilde{u})}\right)^{\prime}\right|\quad(\text{by \eqref{lem5.3_eq2} and \eqref{lem5.3_eq3}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2}{b_{3}}\frac{|A-\tilde{A}|}{A}(1+\tilde{u})^{2}(1+u)\left|\left(\frac{1}{\phi(\tilde{u})}\right)^{\prime}\right|+\frac{2}{b_{3}}\frac{|B-\tilde{B}|}{A}(1+\tilde{u})(1+u)^{2}\left|\left(\frac{1}{1-\phi(\tilde{u})}\right)^{\prime}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{b_{2}}{b_{3}}\frac{|A-\tilde{A}|}{A}(1+\tilde{u})^{2}(1+u)(1+\tilde{u})^{-2}+\frac{2b_{1}}{b_{3}}\frac{|B-\tilde{B}|}{B}(1+\tilde{u})(1+u)^{2}\frac{\left(\frac{1}{\phi(u)}\right)^{\prime}}{\left(\frac{1}{1-\phi(u)}\right)^{\prime}}\quad(\text{by Condition \ref{condition:sigmoid} and \eqref{lem5.3_eq4}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{b_{2}}{b_{3}}\frac{|A-\tilde{A}|}{A}(1+u)+\frac{b_{1}b_{2}}{b_{3}}\frac{|B-\tilde{B}|}{B}(1+\tilde{u})(1+u)^{2}(1+u)^{-2}\frac{1}{\left(\frac{1}{1-\phi(0)}\right)^{\prime}}\quad(\text{by Lemma \ref{lem_u} and Condition \ref{condition:sigmoid}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{b_{2}}{b_{3}}\frac{|A-\tilde{A}|}{A}(1+u)+\frac{2b_{1}b_{2}}{b_{3}^{2}}\frac{|B-\tilde{B}|}{B}(1+\tilde{u})\quad(\text{by Lemma \ref{lem_u}})\kern 5.0pt.

(50)

Hence by Lemma
B.16
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{1-p}-\frac{1}{1-\tilde{p}}\right|\leq b_{1}|u-\tilde{u}|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{b_{1}b_{2}}{b_{3}}\frac{|A-\tilde{A}|}{A}(1+u)+\frac{2b_{1}^{2}b_{2}}{b_{3}^{2}}\frac{|B-\tilde{B}|}{B}(1+\tilde{u})

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-p}-\frac{1}{1-1/2}\geq\frac{b_{3}}{2}(u-0)\kern 5.0pt.

(51)

We can finally obtain from (
(1)
) that

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{1-p}-\frac{1}{1-\tilde{p}}\right|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{b_{1}b_{2}}{b_{3}}\frac{|A-\tilde{A}|}{A}(1+u)+\frac{2b_{1}^{2}b_{2}}{b_{3}^{2}}\frac{|B-\tilde{B}|}{B}(1+\tilde{u})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{b_{1}b_{2}}{b_{3}}\frac{|A-\tilde{A}|}{A}\left[1+\frac{2}{b_{3}}\left(\frac{1}{1-p}-2\right)\right]+\frac{2b_{1}^{2}b_{2}}{b_{3}^{2}}\frac{|B-\tilde{B}|}{B}\left[1+\frac{2}{b_{3}}\left(\frac{1}{1-\tilde{p}}-2\right)\right]\quad(\text{by \eqref{lem5.3_eq6}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{b_{1}b_{2}}{b_{3}}\frac{|A-\tilde{A}|}{A}\left[1+\frac{2}{b_{3}}\left(\frac{p}{1-p}-1\right)\right]+\frac{2b_{1}^{2}b_{2}}{b_{3}^{2}}\frac{|B-\tilde{B}|}{B}\left[1+\frac{2}{b_{3}}\left(\frac{\tilde{p}}{1-\tilde{p}}-1\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{b_{1}b_{2}}{b_{3}}\left(1+\frac{2}{b_{3}}\right)\frac{|A-\tilde{A}|}{A}\frac{p}{1-p}+\frac{2b_{1}^{2}b_{2}}{b_{3}^{2}}\left(1+\frac{2}{b_{3}}\right)\frac{|B-\tilde{B}|}{B}\frac{\tilde{p}}{1-\tilde{p}}\kern 5.0pt.

By similar method as in (
(1)
) and Lemma
B.16
, we can also derive

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{\tilde{p}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{b_{2}}{2}\cdot\frac{|u-\tilde{u}|}{(1+u)(1+\tilde{u})}\quad(\text{Lemma \ref{lem_u}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{b_{2}}{2(1+u)(1+\tilde{u})}\left[\frac{b_{2}}{b_{3}}\frac{|A-\tilde{A}|}{A}(1+u)+\frac{2}{b_{3}}\frac{|B-\tilde{B}|}{A}(1+\tilde{u})(1+u)^{2}\left|\left(\frac{1}{1-\phi(\tilde{u})}\right)^{\prime}\right|\right]\quad(\text{similar proof as in \eqref{lem5.3_eq5}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{b_{2}^{2}}{2b_{3}}\frac{|A-\tilde{A}|}{A}+\frac{b_{1}b_{2}}{b_{3}}\frac{|B-\tilde{B}|}{A}(1+u)\quad(\text{Lemma \ref{lem_u}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{b_{2}^{2}}{2b_{3}}\frac{|A-\tilde{A}|}{A}+\frac{b_{1}b_{2}}{b_{3}}\left(1+\frac{2}{b_{3}}\right)\frac{|B-\tilde{B}|}{A}\frac{p}{1-p}\quad(\text{by \eqref{lem5.3_eq6}})\kern 5.0pt.

(2)

Let
q=1-p
and
\tilde{q}=1-\tilde{p}
.
Then
q
and
\tilde{q}
satisfy:

\displaystyle-\frac{A}{(1-q)^{2}}+\frac{B}{q^{2}}+\Psi^{\prime}(1-q)

\displaystyle=0\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle-\frac{\tilde{A}}{(1-\tilde{q})^{2}}+\frac{\tilde{B}}{\tilde{q}^{2}}+\Psi^{\prime}(1-\tilde{q})

\displaystyle=0\kern 5.0pt.

Since by Condition
1
,
\phi^{-1}(p)=-\phi^{-1}(1-p)
, we have
\Psi(p)=\Psi(1-p)
.
This implies that
\Psi^{\prime}(1-q)=-\Psi^{\prime}(q)
and
\Psi^{\prime}(1-\tilde{q})=-\Psi^{\prime}(\tilde{q})
.
Hence by the proof in (1), the result is verified.

∎

When
p_{t}
and
\bar{p}_{t}
are not both greater than
1/2
or both smaller than
1/2
, it is not possible to directly bound the difference between their inverse probabilities.
However, when the two first-order equations are close, both probabilities should be close to
1/2
.
Therefore, we use
1/2
as an intermediate comparator and bound their difference in the following lemma.

Lemma C.2
.

Let
A,B
be positive constants. Suppose
p
satisfy:

\displaystyle-\frac{A}{p^{2}}+\frac{B}{(1-p)^{2}}+\Psi^{\prime}(p)

\displaystyle=0\kern 5.0pt.

(1)

If
B\leq A
, then under Condition
1
,
p
satisfies
0<\frac{1}{1-p}-2\leq\frac{b_{1}b_{2}}{b_{3}}\cdot\frac{A-B}{B}
.

(2)

If
A\leq B
, then under Condition
1
,
p
satisfies
0<\frac{1}{p}-2\leq\frac{b_{1}b_{2}}{b_{3}}\cdot\frac{B-A}{A}
.

Proof.

(1)

For
u=\phi^{-1}(p)\geq 0
and
\tilde{u}=\phi^{-1}(1/2)=0
, by Condition
1
it is easy to see that
u
and
\tilde{u}
satisfy:

\displaystyle A\left(\frac{1}{\phi(u)}\right)^{\prime}+B\left(\frac{1}{1-\phi(u)}\right)^{\prime}+u+3u^{2}=

\displaystyle 0\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle B\left(\frac{1}{\phi(\tilde{u})}\right)^{\prime}+B\left(\frac{1}{1-\phi(\tilde{u})}\right)^{\prime}+\tilde{u}+3\tilde{u}^{2}=

\displaystyle 0\kern 5.0pt.

(52)

By subtracting the first equation in (
(1)
) from the second equation, we have

\displaystyle A\left[\left(\frac{1}{\phi(u)}\right)^{\prime}-\left(\frac{1}{\phi(\tilde{u})}\right)^{\prime}\right]+(A-B)\left(\frac{1}{\phi(\tilde{u})}\right)^{\prime}+B\left[\left(\frac{1}{1-\phi(u)}\right)^{\prime}-\left(\frac{1}{1-\phi(\tilde{u})}\right)^{\prime}\right]

\displaystyle+(u-\tilde{u})(1+3u+3\tilde{u})=0\kern 5.0pt.

Since
A\left[\left(\frac{1}{\phi(u)}\right)^{\prime}-\left(\frac{1}{\phi(\tilde{u})}\right)^{\prime}\right]\geq 0
,
B\left[\left(\frac{1}{1-\phi(u)}\right)^{\prime}-\left(\frac{1}{1-\phi(\tilde{u})}\right)^{\prime}\right]\geq 0
and
(u-\tilde{u})(1+3u+3\tilde{u})\geq 0
, by Lemma
B.16
we have

\displaystyle\left|A\left[\left(\frac{1}{\phi(u)}\right)^{\prime}-\left(\frac{1}{\phi(\tilde{u})}\right)^{\prime}\right]+B\left[\left(\frac{1}{1-\phi(u)}\right)^{\prime}-\left(\frac{1}{1-\phi(\tilde{u})}\right)^{\prime}\right]+\eta^{-1}(u-\tilde{u})(1+3u+3\tilde{u})\right|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle A\left|\left(\frac{1}{\phi(u)}\right)^{\prime}-\left(\frac{1}{\phi(\tilde{u})}\right)^{\prime}\right|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{b_{3}}{2}\frac{A}{(1+\tilde{u})(1+u)(1+\tilde{u}\land u)}\cdot|u-\tilde{u}|\kern 5.0pt.

Hence by Lemma
B.16
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-p}-\frac{1}{1-1/2}\leq

\displaystyle b_{1}(u-\tilde{u})\quad(\text{Lemma \ref{lem_u}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle b_{1}\left(\frac{b_{3}}{2}\frac{B}{(1+\tilde{u})(1+u)(1+\tilde{u}\land u)}\right)^{-1}(A-B)\left|\left(\frac{1}{\phi(u)}\right)^{\prime}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{b_{1}b_{2}}{b_{3}}\frac{A-B}{B}(1+u)^{2}(1+\tilde{u})(1+u)^{-2}\quad(\text{Lemma \ref{lem_u}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{b_{1}b_{2}}{b_{3}}\cdot\frac{A-B}{B}\quad(\text{since $\tilde{u}=0$})\kern 5.0pt.

(2)

Let
q=1-p
. Then
q
satisfies

\displaystyle-\frac{B}{q^{2}}+\frac{A}{(1-q)^{2}}+\Psi^{\prime}(q)

\displaystyle=0\kern 5.0pt.

By the same method as in (1) we can prove that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{p}-2=\frac{1}{1-q}-2\leq\frac{b_{1}b_{2}}{b_{3}}\cdot\frac{B-A}{A}\kern 5.0pt.

∎

Lemma
C.1
and Lemma
C.2
together imply that controlling the difference between the inverse probabilities is equivalent to bounding the difference between the corresponding first-order equations.
By the definitions of
p_{t}
and
\bar{p}_{t}
, this reduces to bounding the difference between the estimated squared residuals and their expectations, which can be achieved by controlling the variance of the estimated squared residuals.
The following lemma establishes such variance bounds.
We denote
\widehat{a}_{t}(k)=\eta_{t}\widehat{A}_{t}(k)
and
a_{t}(k)=\eta_{t}\mathrm{E}\widehat{A}_{t}(k)
for
k\in\{0,1\}
and any
t\in[T]
.

Lemma C.3
.

Suppose
T
is large enough. Under Assumptions
1
-
3
and Condition
1
, for any
t\in[T]
, the variance of
\widehat{a}_{t}(1)
and
\widehat{a}_{t}(0)
can be bounded by:

\displaystyle\Varsym\lparen\widehat{a}_{t}(1)\rparen\leq

\displaystyle C(\eta_{t}T)^{1/2}\log^{2}(\eta_{t}T)(2+b_{1}(b_{2}/6)^{1/4}a_{t}^{1/4}(0))^{25/16}\kern 5.0pt,

\displaystyle\Varsym\lparen\widehat{a}_{t}(0)\rparen\leq

\displaystyle C(\eta_{t}T)^{1/2}\log^{2}(\eta_{t}T)(2+b_{1}(b_{2}/6)^{1/4}a_{t}^{1/4}(1))^{25/16}\kern 5.0pt,

where
C\triangleq\left(64\xi_{5/4}+5248\right)c_{1}^{4}(\gamma_{0}\vee c_{2}\vee 1)^{2}>0
is a constant.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we only prove the first inequality.
By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and AM-GM inequality, the variance of
\widehat{A}_{t}(1)
can be bounded by:

\displaystyle\Varsym\lparen\widehat{A}_{t}(1)\rparen

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{Var}\left[\sum_{s=1}^{t}\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=1]}{p_{s}}\cdot\left(y_{s}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{Var}\left[\sum_{s=1}^{t}\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=1]}{p_{s}}-1\right)\cdot\left(y_{s}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}+\sum_{s=1}^{t}\left(y_{s}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\operatorname{Var}\left[\sum_{s=1}^{t}\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=1]}{p_{s}}-1\right)\cdot\left(y_{s}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}\right]+2\operatorname{Var}\left[\sum_{s=1}^{t}\left(y_{s}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle 2S_{1}+2S_{2}\kern 5.0pt.

(53)

For term
S_{1}
, using inequality
(x+y+z)^{4}\leq 27x^{4}+27y^{4}+27z^{4}
and the law of total variance, we have

\displaystyle S_{1}=

\displaystyle\operatorname{Var}\left[\sum_{s=1}^{t}\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=1]}{p_{s}}-1\right)\cdot\left(y_{s}(1)-\langle x_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{t}\operatorname{Var}\left[\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=1]}{p_{s}}-1\right)\cdot\left(y_{s}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2\sum_{1\leq s_{1}<s_{2}\leq t}\operatorname{Cov}\left(\left[\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s_{1}}=1]}{p_{s_{1}}}-1\right]\cdot\left(y_{s_{1}}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s_{1}},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s_{1}}(1)\rangle\right)^{2},\left[\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s_{2}}=1]}{p_{s_{2}}}-1\right]\cdot\left(y_{s_{2}}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s_{2}},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s_{2}}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{t}\operatorname{Var}\left[\mathrm{E}\left[\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=1]}{p_{s}}-1\right)\cdot\left(y_{s}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}\Bigg|\mathcal{F}_{s-1}\right]\right]

\displaystyle+\sum_{s=1}^{t}\mathrm{E}\left[\operatorname{Var}\left[\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s}=1]}{p_{s}}-1\right)\cdot\left(y_{s}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}\Bigg|\mathcal{F}_{s-1}\right]\right]

\displaystyle+2\sum_{1\leq s_{1}<s_{2}\leq t}\operatorname{Cov}\Bigg(\mathrm{E}\left[\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s_{1}}=1]}{p_{s_{1}}}-1\right)\cdot\left(y_{s_{1}}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s_{1}},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s_{1}}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}\Bigg|\mathcal{F}_{s_{2}-1}\right],

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\mathrm{E}\left[\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s_{2}}=1]}{p_{s_{2}}}-1\right)\cdot\left(y_{s_{2}}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s_{2}},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s_{2}}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}\Bigg|\mathcal{F}_{s_{2}-1}\right]\Bigg)

\displaystyle+2\sum_{1\leq s_{1}<s_{2}\leq t}\mathrm{E}\Bigg[\operatorname{Cov}\Bigg(\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s_{1}}=1]}{p_{s_{1}}}-1\right)\cdot\left(y_{s_{1}}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s_{1}},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s_{1}}(1)\rangle\right)^{2},

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\left(\frac{\mathbf{1}[Z_{s_{2}}=1]}{p_{s_{2}}}-1\right)\cdot\left(y_{s_{2}}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s_{2}},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s_{2}}(1)\rangle\right)^{2}\Bigg|\mathcal{F}_{s_{2}-1}\Bigg)\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{t}\mathrm{E}\left[\frac{1-p_{s}}{p_{s}}\left(y_{s}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s}(1)\rangle\right)^{4}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{t}\mathrm{E}\left[\frac{1}{p_{s}}\left(y_{s}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s}(1)\rangle\right)^{4}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{t}\mathrm{E}\left[\frac{1}{p_{s}}\left(y_{s}(1)-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s}^{*}(1)\rangle-\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s}(1)-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}_{s}(1)\rangle\right)^{4}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 27\sum_{s=1}^{t}y_{s}^{4}(1)\mathrm{E}\left[\frac{1}{p_{s}}\right]+27\sum_{s=1}^{t}\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s}^{*}(1)\rangle^{4}\mathrm{E}\left[\frac{1}{p_{s}}\right]+27\sum_{s=1}^{t}\mathrm{E}\left[\frac{1}{p_{s}}\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{s},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{s}(1)-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}_{s}(1)\rangle^{4}\right]

\displaystyle\triangleq

\displaystyle 27S_{1,1}+27S_{1,2}+27S_{1,3}\kern 5.0pt.

(54)

By Corollary
4.15
, Corollary
B.17
and Assumption
1
,
S_{1,1}
can be bounded as:

\displaystyle S_{1,1}\lesssim T\cdot T^{1/8}=T^{9/8}=o(T^{5/4}R_{t}^{3})\kern 5.0pt.

(55)

By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, Corollary
B.4
and Assumption
1
, for any
t\in[T]
we have

\displaystyle\langle\boldsymbol{x}_{t},\boldsymbol{\beta}^{*}_{t}(1)\rangle^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}\Pi_{t,s}y_{s}(1)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}\Pi_{t,s}^{2}\right)\left(\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}y_{s}^{2}(1)\right)~~~~(\text{Cauchy-Schwarz inequality})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\left(\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}+\eta_{t}^{-1}\boldsymbol{I}_{d}\right)^{-1}\boldsymbol{x}_{s}\boldsymbol{x}_{s}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\left(\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}^{{\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\displaystyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\textstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptstyle\intercal$}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\intercal$}}}}\boldsymbol{X}_{t-1}+\eta_{t}^{-1}\boldsymbol{I}_{d}\right)^{-1}\boldsymbol{x}_{t}\right)\left(\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}y_{s}^{2}(1)\right)

\displaystyle=
